市場焦點
美國
• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8
美國零售柴油價格與通膨預期走勢對比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。

顧
美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響
資料來源：美國銀行，2026.7
資料來源：復華投信，2026.9

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

科技產業AI資本支出規模最大企業
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.23

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。

### Page 3

Largan Precision (3008.TW)
08 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Seeing multiple generation and years opportunities — Compared with these
broader solutions providers, Largan remains primarily as a high-precision
manufacturing and assembly specialist. Management described its current
business relationship as closer to contract manufacturing, with direct discussions
with end customers focused mainly on next-generation products. On product
architecture, the initial 2027 commercial project remains focused on single-row FA

using grating coupling (GC), while multi-row and multilayer configurations remain in
sampling and are primarily associated with 2028 programs. Management believes
its FA/FAU solution is broadly applicable to both CPO and NPO when the underlying
optical interface remains similar, because the primary difference is the placement of
the optical package. Largan’s primary differentiation is not the FA design itself but
its ability to manufacture highly precise assembly using imperfect input

components.
Automation and Yield Control Are the Real Competitive
Advantages
During the earnings call, Largan’s management emphasized that almost all critical
FA/FAU alignment equipment is developed internally, including machinery and
control software. This enables faster equipment modification, process
optimization and troubleshooting compared with labor-intensive assembly.
Automated production also offers better traceability and root-cause analysis when

yield problems emerge. Management stated that customer feedback is positive
and the FAU certification process progressing well. The key competitive advantage,
in our view, is the ability to combine submicron accuracy, high throughput and
reproducible yields at scale, rather than precision alone.
In our view, the earnings call supports a more constructive medium-term view of
Largan’s CPO opportunities than our previous assessment, particularly given the

apparent completion of initial FA qualification, customer interest in integrated FAU
assembly and a clearer path toward commercial production in 2H27. Moving from
standalone FA to FAU and potentially carrier assembly could materially increase
revenue content per optical connection, while the company’s automated
manufacturing capabilities may support differentiation in increasingly complex,
high-density optical architectures. The next growth opportunity could emerge from

multi-row FA and higher-channel-count optical solutions beginning in 2028,
particularly as AI networking bandwidth moves beyond current-generation
platforms.
Maintaining Buy on better FAU visibility
Given the weak smartphone order flow and GM pressure in the near term, we revise
down our net income projections by 9%/29% for 2026/2027, but slightly raise our
2028 net income projection by 1% given the solid growth opportunity in FAU
business. However, we think Largan is transitioning from a smartphone optics

manufacturer into a potential high-precision optical interconnect assembly
supplier. As they are moving to FAU not just FA; we assume higher revenue
contribution for 2027 /2028 of 28%/ 44% considering Largan could get 15%/30%
market share.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

better visibility on FAU progress.

### Page 8

Largan Precision (3008.TW)
08 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Largan Precision
Company description
Largan is a plastic/glass/hybrid lens supplier in Taiwan with end-applications
including phone cameras, DSCs, MFPs, and tablets. Its new development into
FAU business should server as a long term growth opportunity.
Investment strategy
We rate Largan a Buy as we expect its core iPhone business to remain resilient
and its co-packaged optics (CPO) development to provide strong upside

potential in the longer term. We believe Largan could be a long-term winner in
the lens market driven by: 1) its technology leadership; 2) intellectual property
(IP) protection; and 3) higher yield vs. competitors. In the longer term, we
believe spec migration could keep Largan ahead of competitors.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$8,500 for Largan shares is based on 33x 2027 /28E
EPS. We are using 2027 /28E EPS average as we see new Fiber Array + PMLA

revenue to kick in from next year. Our 33x PER is higher than its historical 5-
year PER range, which we think is justified by its better order visibility proven
by its recent strong capacity expansion plan, and we expect its CPO business
engagement should provide a strong long-term growth opportunity.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Chenbro (8210.TW): Chassis and Racks in expansion on AI infrastructure 
upcycle; Neutral
12 October 2026 | 2:24AM HKT
We are positive on Chenbro’s leading market position in AI server chassis, riding on AI 
capex across US and China cloud service providers (CSPs), and expanding to full rack 
business (e.g. noise-cancellation racks, liquid cooling CDU racks, etc.). Chenbro’s 
revenues in Sep increased by 1% MoM, and we expect the sequential increase to 
continue in Oct-Dec, bringing 4Q26E revenue growth to +58% YoY / +44% QoQ. 
Catalysts in 4Q26 include: (1) major ASIC AI servers customers’ new models ramp-up, 
(2) rack business expansion on noise-cancellation racks ramping up and expansion to 
liquid cooling CDU racks, (3) AI servers powered by NVIDIA (e.g. HGX, MGX) in 
expansion with new chipset platforms, and (4) ongoing capacity expansion to 
support the revenues growth. Maintain Neutral on fair valuation.

Earnings revision

We lower net income by 8%/3%/4% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues to reflect the 
less-than-expected shipment of AI servers chassis and the 3Q26 revenues miss, which 
were 22%/15% lower than our estimates / Bloomberg consensus. We lower revenues by 
7%/5%/6% in 2026-28E.

Compared to Bloomberg consensus, we are 1%/12%/27% ahead on 2026E/ 27E/28E 
net income, reflecting our positive view on Chenbro’s revenues expansion.

### Page 3

Valuation

We continue to use near-term P/E to derive our 12m TP . We derive our target P/E 
multiple from (1) peers’ avg. ratio of trading P/E to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY 
and OPM), which is at 0.3x, and (2) Chenbro’s forward year fundamentals: 2027-28E NI 
YoY now at 47% on avg. and OPM is at 26.8% on avg. Our new target P/E multiple is at 
21.2x (vs. 20.2x of our previous TP implied 2027E P/E). Our previous target P/E multiple 
to forward year fundamentals was also 0.3x. Our new target P/E multiple of 21.2x is 
between the company’s avg.+1stv. P/E of 17x and peak P/E of 26x, reflecting our 
positive view on its expansion toward AI servers. Accordingly, we raise our target price 
by 1.7% to NT$1,507 (21.2x 2027E P/E) from NT$1,482.

### Page 4

Price Target Risks and Methodology: Chenbro 
Valuation: We use near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price for Chenbro, 
consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of NT$1,507 
on a target P/E multiple of 21.2x on forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E of 21.2x is 
derived from the peers’ avg. ratio of trading P/E to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY 
and OPM). We have a Neutral rating on Chenbro on fair valuation. Key upside / 
downside risks: faster / slower-than-expected AI infrastructure investments, faster / 
slower-than-expected rack business ramp-up, or healthier / fiercer-than-expected 
competition.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

M
Update
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Solid 2027 and 2028 outlook
with potential catalysts; OW
What's new? Ahead of Alchip's 3Q26 earnings release on November 6, investors are
looking for updates on the company's 2027 and 2028 revenue outlook, particularly
regarding AWS Trainium programmes and potential new customer wins.
Major projects – AWS Trainium: It appears TSMC is allocating additional 3nm
capacity to AI ASICs and CPUs in 2027. We do not rule out AWS-related revenue

growing more than 70% YoY in 2027, versus our current forecast of 56% YoY
growth. As a result, we remain confident in our 2027 revenue forecast of US$4.4bn.
We believe gross margin can remain sustainable despite the additional revenue
contribution, which would be better than our previous assumption of fixed gross
profit from 3nm Trainium3. For 2028, we forecast a Street-high US$8bn revenue
contribution from 2nm Trainium4. Our supply-chain checks suggest US$8bn–12bn of

revenue is possible if AWS allocates additional volume.
Potential new projects - Google KGD and others:  In  Asia-Pacific Technology: AI
Supply Chain: Google ASIC, who gets what for design services? (24 Aug 2026), we
highlighted key trends for Google TPU v10, including: "KGD (known good die) for the
compute die, which means Google owns the compute-die design and photomask
tools, while retaining flexibility to outsource backend design services for the

compute die." We believe Alchip is a leading candidate for this KGD backend-design
opportunity. In addition, Alchip is pursuing another US HPC customer opportunity.
Our checks suggest a switch-networking product is a possibility, potentially
involving collaboration with Astera Labs and NVLink Fusion.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Alchip Technologies Ltd (3661.TW)
Base case value, derived from a residual income model. We assume the cost of equity is con-
stant at 10.4% (2% risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.4 beta). Our intermediate growth rate and
terminal growth rates are 12.5% and 4%, respectively.
Risks to Upside
n Demand for Chinese GPUs and AI chipsets takes off earlier than expected.
n NRE demand accelerates.

n Alchip wins more US hyperscaler HPC projects.
Risks to Downside
n China's HPC localization progress slows down.
n AI chipset progress is much slower to develop.
n NRE demand decelerates.
n Pricing competition intensifies.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 163: 3443 Nomura20261019

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
Global Unichip Corp   3443.TW 3443 TT
Global Markets Research
19 October 2026
Riding the next megatrend of SoIC; maintain Buy
Several key CSP projects in the pipeline to support growth until 2028-29F
Benefiting from SoIC — the next megatrend in advanced packaging, in our view
GUC shares have rallied ~100% since late July (vs the TAIEX up 11%), driven by a strong 
wave of consensus earnings upward revisions (Fig. 11). This is largely in line with our 
observations highlighted in our 2 Aug report that “consensus expectations were way too low.” 
We believe the Street is starting to acknowledge that there is a strong pipeline of CSP projects 
over the next 2-3 years, including Google CPU (GOOGL US, Not rated; revenue sharply 
higher in 2026 with momentum potentially sustaining into 2027F and 2028F as Axion 3 
doubles silicon content from Axion 2), as well as the other top three US hyperscalers’ ASIC 
and CPU projects along with Tesla’s (TSLA US, Not rated) AI5/AI6.5. GUC not only has 
secured projects from top US hyperscalers, but at the same time acquired additional 
meaningful customers with volume globally, e.g. China auto ASIC and US AI ASIC using a 
SoW approach. However, among all, the one that has caught our attention the most is the 
Google TPU v.10 compute die opportunity (though we believe the turnkey deal is yet to be 
finalized) — not only because the addressable size could be significant, but this business 
opportunity might originate from GUC’s G-link 3D die-on-die IP. (Fig. 1 Fig. 4) We raise our 
2026-28F EPS for GUC by 15%-37% to incorporate business upside from non-Axion projects, 
and raise TP to TWD9,600 (from TWD5,800), based on an unchanged 40x target P/E, while 
rolling forward valuation to the 2027-28F average EPS. We are yet to incorporate overseas 
convertible bonds (ECB) in our model/EPS estimates.
The rising importance of SoIC in advanced packaging should benefit GUC, given its 
leading investments in Glink-3D die-on-die IP among design service peers
SoIC is not new, but it was small before 2026 given limited adoption by AMD MI300 chip (AMD 
US, Not rated) and Apple M-series chip (AAPL US, Not rated). In 2026, nVidia’s (NVDA US, Not 
rated) demand for CPO using TSMC’s (2330 TT, Buy) COUPE (EIC on PIC stacking) has further 
lifted SoIC demand. In our June anchor report, we noted that nVidia’s Feynman could become 
the first GPU-on-GPU stacking SoIC (along with SiC carrier) AI chip. Since then, several US CSP 
ASIC projects have also planned to enable SoIC die stacking in their chip roadmap, according to 
our industry checks. GUC’s Glink-3D IP (see Fig. 1- Fig. 4) appears well positioned to ride this 
trend as it has developed this IP along with TSMC’s roadmap and currently a number of CSPs 
are using its IP to build their test chip – which in the long run could prove to be a good stepping 
stone for GUC to win more services, in our view. Besides, GUC’s SoIC IP would also be 
deployed with TSMC’s scale-up CPO customers (e.g. AyarLabs, Fig. 5).

### Page 3

Company profile
Established in 1998, GUC is one of the leading design service companies in Taiwan with self-developed IPs. TSMC is GUC's single-largest
shareholder with a 35% share.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD9,600 is based on 40x 2027-28F average EPS (40x P/E is its historical mid level). The benchmark index for the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks include 1) fierce ASIC competition, 2) slowdown of key projects and lack of capacity, and 3) more insourcing trend from customers

ESG
GUC has six major ESG strategies to achieve the goals of sustainable development, including corporate governance, innovative R&D, partnership
prosperity, environmental sustainability, friendly workplace, and social participation.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

時碩 (4566  TT)  Trading Buy

2H26 營運明顯增長 ，投資建議 Trading Buy 。  
投 資 建 議 Trading Buy ： 雖 目 前 時碩 (4566 TT) 處於 CB 緘 默 期 ，
未 提 供 明 確 展 望 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 產 品 出 貨 增 長 ，3Q26 營
運 已 明 顯 攀 升 。 考 量 2026 、 2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上 ， 投 資 建 議
Trading Buy ， 目 標 價 92 元 (以 202 7 年 EPS 及 PE  17 倍 評 價 )。  
時 碩 積 極 布 局 高 成 長 潛 力 的 利 基 型 市 場 ， 包 含 液 冷 散 熱 系 統 、
人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 ： 液冷散熱 方面， 時 碩 致 力 於 提 供 一 站 式 的
全 方 位 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 ， 其 產 品 線 涵 蓋 快 接 頭 、 分 歧 管 、 冷 卻
液 分 配 單 元 、 微 通 道 板 及 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 水 冷 板 ， 並 能 提 供 不
鏽 鋼 與 銅 等 多 樣 材 質 。公司 已 成 功 獲 得 美 、 日 系 液 冷 系 統 大 廠
訂單，且於 2H26 放量 出貨 ， 預估 散 熱 產 品 佔 202 6 年 營 收 比 重
有望 接近 10% 。 人 形 機 器 人方 面 ， 時 碩 積 極 拓 展 此 領 域 的 關 鍵
零 組 件 業 務 ， 產 品 涵 蓋 諧 波 減 速 機 關 鍵 零 組 件 、 機 器 手 臂 關 節
精 密 機 械 加 工 零 件 。 時 碩 目 前 已 打 入 美 國 與 中 國 廠 商 的 供 應 鏈
體 系 ； 中 國 無 錫 廠 藉 由 地 緣 優 勢 積 極 切 入 關 節 零 件 製 造 。 泰 國
春 武 里 新 廠也 規 劃 將 產 線 逐 步 延 伸 至 機 器 人 相 關 應 用 領 域 。 
3Q26 營 收 明 顯 躍 升，預估 2026 、2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上：雖 1H26
獲 利 表 現 平 平 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 出 貨 明 顯 增 長 帶 動 ，
3Q26 營收 18.18 億元， QoQ+23.8 6%、 YoY+43.23% ； 且 出 貨 動
能 有 望 延 伸 至 4Q26 。2026 年 方 面 ，汽 車 、 航 太 、 工 業 三 大 領 域
皆有望較 2025 年 增 長。汽 車 應 用 方 面， 雖 中 國 市 場 競 爭 仍 然 激
烈，但 因 切 入 新 能 源 車 領 域 且 客 戶 需 求 帶 動 下，營 收穩 定 增 長。
航太領域 方面， 天 陽 航 太 已 通 過 多 項 航 太 認 證 ， 成 功 打 入 全 球
前 兩 大 飛 機 製 造 商 及 Tier 1 供 應 商 ， 預 估 營 收 將維 持 高 速 成 長
態勢 。 工 業 應 用 方 面 ， 既 有 領 域 有 望 較2025 年 改 善 ， 且 新 成 長
動 能 將 來 自 於 液 冷 散 熱 相 關 產 品 及 人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 。 預 估
2026 年 營 收 66.00 億 元 ，YoY+ 32.62 %， 毛 利 率 維 持 高 檔 ， 營 業

並 維 持 完 善 的 資 訊 安 全 管 理 以 避 免 資 訊 外 洩。在 治 理 面，時 碩 建 構 包 含 獨 立
董 事 與 女 性 董 事 的 董 事 會 結 構，設 置 薪 酬 與 審 計 等 功 能 性 委 員 會，訂 定 涵 蓋

6 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15 %

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

楼市回暖信号持续释放！ 9 ⽉ 70 城数据显⽰，⼀线城市新房、⼆⼿房价格环⽐双双上涨。其
中，上海⼆⼿房房价同⽐上涨 0.8% ；环⽐上涨城市个数⼤幅增加，⼆线新房价格更实现由
跌转平。各线城市同⽐降幅全⾯收窄，政策效果正逐步显现。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，国家统计局公布， 2026 年 9 ⽉份， 70 个⼤中城市中，⼀线城市商品住宅销售价格环
⽐继续上涨，⼆三线城市环⽐降幅总体收窄，⼀⼆三线城市同⽐降幅均收窄，环⽐上涨城市个
数⽐上⽉增加。
⼆⼿房：⼀线城市房价环⽐涨幅扩⼤，环⽐上涨城市个数⽐上⽉增加
环⽐看， 9 ⽉份，⼀线城市房价环⽐涨幅扩⼤ 0.1 个百分点，价格环⽐上涨城市有 9 个，⽐上⽉增
加 6 个。
同⽐看， 9 ⽉份，各线城市⼆⼿房同⽐售价降幅均有所收窄。
具体看，⼀线城市中：
⼀线城市⼆⼿住宅销售价格环⽐上涨 0.2% ，涨幅⽐上⽉扩⼤ 0.1 个百分点。
⼆线城市⼆⼿住宅销售价格环⽐下降 0.2% ，降幅⽐上⽉收窄 0.1 个百分点。
三线城市⼆⼿住宅销售价格环⽐下降 0.3% ，降幅与上⽉相同。

70 个⼤中城市中，⼆⼿住宅销售价格环⽐上涨城市有 9 个，⽐上⽉增加 6 个。
⼀线城市⼆⼿住宅销售价格同⽐下降 1.5% ，降幅⽐上⽉收窄 1.2 个百分点。
⼆、三线城市⼆⼿住宅销售价格同⽐分别下降 4.5% 和 5.4% ，降幅分别收窄 0.4 个和 0.2 个百分
点。

9 月金融数据点评

关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响   
9 月新增社融和新增信贷均好于万得一致预期；企业融资需求分层，信用度
较高的企业以企业债为代表的融资需求下降，依赖贷款渠道的企业贷款 9 月
表现相对更好；居民部门加杠杆意愿不强；受资本市场波动影响，存款搬家
有变化的趋势；关注海外加息周期和高利率环境对国内金融条件和货币政策
的潜在影响。 
 9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，较去年同期多增 192 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.89 万亿元，
高于万得一致预期的 3.13 万亿元。9 月社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较 8 月下降了
0.07 个百分点，基本持平于万得一致预期的 7.06%。9 月社融口径新增人民币贷
款 1.27 万亿元，较去年同期少增 3342 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.22 万亿元。 
 9 月新增社融较去年同期小幅多增，主要原因是政府债券较去年同期多增了 3438
亿元，同时委托贷款和外币贷款也较去年同期有所多增；从当月新增规模看，新
增融资规模较大的项目，主要是政府债券、人民币贷款和汇票，当月少增的是信
托贷款和企业债。 值得关注的是9 月股票融资表现依然较好， 当月新增658 亿元，
较去年同期也有小幅上升， 但企业债少增425 亿元， 表明企业融资需求依然不强。 
 从社融存量结构来看，2026 年 9 月与 8 月相比，占比上升的有政府债券（上升
0.17 个百分点）和汇票（上升 0.04 个百分点），占比下降的有股票融资（下降
0.01 个百分点）、信托贷款（下降 0.02 个百分点）、企业债（下降 0.08 个百分
点）和人民币贷款（下降 0.16 个百分点）。与去年同期相比，9 月占比上升较多
的是政府债券（上升 1.30 个百分点）和企业债（上升 0.16 个百分点），占比下
降较多的是人民币贷款 （下降1.31 个百分点） 和委托贷款 （下降0.13 个百分点） 。
虽然新增企业债融资规模少增，但企业债融资占比仍有上升趋势。 
 9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较 8 月下降 0.3 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较 8 月
下降 0.9 个百分点；M0 同比增长 10.6%，较 8 月下降 0.6 个百分点。9 月活期存
款（M1-M0）环比上升 0.79%，定期存款（M2-M1）环比上升 0.76%，与去年同
期相比，活期存款环比增速偏低，定期存款环比增速偏高，关注资本市场指数波
动对存款变化趋势的影响。 
 9 月关注非银存款回流。9 月金融机构新增存款 1.77 万亿元，多增的主要是居民
存款 2.40 万亿元和企业存款 1.31 万亿元，财政存款和非银存款均少增；与去年
同期相比，9 月新增存款少增 4400 亿元，其中非银存款少增 8500 亿元，居民存
款少增 5600 亿元。9 月新增存款不弱， 但与去年同期相较不强， 其中值得关注的
细节较多：一是企业存款多增，一定程度上印证了企业融资需求不强的原因，二
是居民存款较去年同期少增，关注居民收入增速变化情况，三是财政存款较去年
同期多增， 关注财政支出是否偏慢， 以及对固投增速的影响， 四是非银存款偏弱，
可能代表投资者对资本市场的态度短期偏谨慎。 
 9 月实体经济融资需求延续偏弱。9 月金融机构口径新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，
略高于万得一致预期的 9880 亿元。 其中， 新增短贷及票据3200 亿元， 中长贷9774
亿元， 企业贷1.29 万亿元。 我们认为有三处值得关注， 一是虽然新增贷款超预期，
但是较去年同期少增，主要是居民贷款少增较多，二是新增企业贷款较多，与企
业债发行融资需求偏弱有所不同，三是居民短贷和企业短贷均较去年同期少增较
多。我们认为贷款数据指向了居民部门加杠杆需求不强，以及企业部门内部出现
分化，其中信用等级较高的企业融资需求不强，但依靠信贷的企业中长期贷款需
求仍在。 
 关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响。9 月底以来，央行货币政策出台了三
项主要调整，一是央行等八部门联合印发《关于金融支持服务业扩能提质的指导
意见》，二是 9 月 29 日发布调整完善若干货币政策工具，包括下调抵押补充贷
款（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，将“六张网”建设纳入抵押补充贷款支持领域，及
再贷款扩容等，三是强化财政金融政策协同，实施居民购房贷款贴息政策。整体
来看适度宽松的货币政策仍在发力，但货币政策继续宽松的约束也在加强：从国
内方面看，9 月企业新发放贷款加权平均利率略低于 3%，比上年同期低约 0.2 个
百分点，个人住房新发放贷款加权平均利率约为 3.1%，与上年同期基本持平，9
月底银行间隔夜拆借利率在 1.36%的较低水平，十年期国债利率在 1.68%的较低
水平，人民币对美元汇率增长 10 月初升值突破 6.7；从海外看，市场方面，9 月
底十年美债收益率达到 5.29%的较高水平，同时美联储已于 9 月落地一次加息，
并且纪要显示年内仍将有一次加息。我们认为下一阶段需要关注“适度宽松”的
货币政策可能受外部环境影响，出现国内金融条件“被动收缩”的情况。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济回落速度偏快；国际局势复杂化。

财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 234: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields
Date:    4 hours ago
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears
and Lowers Bond Yields
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 15 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash

I. Treasury Yields
US Treasury yields fell sharply today, extending Wednesday's retreat from
intraday highs. The 10-year yield dropped 9bps to 5.10%, while the 30-
year fell 10bps to 5.43% (chart).

### Page 2

Even the 2-year yield declined slightly to 4.77% (chart).
Remarkably, this broad-based decline occurred despite two hotter-than-
expected inflation reports. Normally, such surprises would push Treasury
yields higher as investors priced in a more hawkish Fed.
So, what's going on? We think the bond market is focusing on the
sustainability of the AI investment boom. That issue came to the fore this
week as CoreWeave's bonds plunged below 80 cents on the dollar and

debt financing major data center projects also tumbled. A new Gartner
report estimates AI data center operators will need $8.5 trillion in revenues
through 2030 to cover costs and earn healthy returns, versus projected
revenues of "just" $3.6 trillion.

### Page 3

Why does this matter? The AI buildout has become a major engine of US
economic growth. But doubts about future returns are pushing up
borrowing costs, potentially constraining access to capital and ultimately
slowing investment spending.
As a result, investors may be lowering their economic growth expectations
amid concerns that the AI boom could slow, pushing Treasury yields lower.
This reinforces our longstanding view that the earlier surge in Treasury

yields largely reflected stronger-than-expected nominal GDP growth
(chart).
We are nowhere near ready to write off the AI investment boom. Demand
for compute continues to outpace supply, while rising adoption should fuel
further investment. Indeed, just five hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet,
Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle) are expected to spend a combined $892
billion this year and $1.1 trillion next year. Their strong balance sheets and
cash flow generation make their spending plans relatively insensitive to

higher borrowing costs. While heavily indebted AI infrastructure providers
may struggle, we expect the hyperscalers to keep spending and the
Roaring 2020s to keep roaring.
II. Inflation
Let's take a closer look at September's hotter-than-expected PPI report
and what it tells us about inflationary pressures in the pipeline.

### Page 4

(1) PPI for final demand. September's PPI final demand surged 1.3%
m/m, largely reflecting a 10.8% spike in energy prices. However, even
excluding the volatile food, energy, and trade services components,
producer prices still rose 0.5%, suggesting that inflationary pressures
remain broad-based.
On a y/y basis, core PPI final demand rose to 5.0%, with services and
goods prices rising 5.0% and 5.2%, respectively (chart).
(2) PPI for consumption. The PPI for personal consumption, a key input

into the Fed’s preferred PCED inflation gauge, surged by 1.3% m/m in
September (chart). Excluding food and energy, it was up 0.6%.

### Page 5

Core PPI personal consumption rose to 4.6% y/y and remained well
above both core PCED and core CPI inflation, suggesting that upside
risks to consumer inflation remain elevated (chart).
(3) Supercore inflation. The PPI supercore inflation rate rose to 4.6% y/y
in September (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Core PCED. With September CPI and PPI now in hand, most
forecasters expect core PCED inflation to rise 0.3% m/m in September,
which would lift the y/y rate from 3.0% to 3.1%. The Cleveland Fed’s
Inflation Nowcasting model projects 0.28% m/m and 3.05% y/y,
respectively.
III. Booming Economy
For now, the US economy shows no signs of slowing down. Consider the
latest data points:
(1) Jobless claims. Initial jobless claims edged up slightly to 199,000 in

the week ended October 10, the fifth straight week below 200,000. That is
the longest streak since 1969! Continuing claims declined to 1.71 million,
dragging down the 4-week moving average to the lowest level since April
2023 (chart).

### Page 7

(2) Retail sales. September retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, partly driven by a
6.4% surge in gasoline station sales amid elevated pump prices (chart).
Still, eight of the 13 major categories posted gains. Sales at food services
and drinking places, the only services category and a key discretionary
spending indicator, jumped 1.1% m/m, their eighth consecutive monthly
gain. They rose 7.0% y/y, the strongest increase since March 2023.
(3) GDP. Reflecting today's retail sales report, the Atlanta Fed’s GDPNow

model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at 3.6% (saar), unchanged from
October 8 (chart). Consumer spending is projected to rise 3.6%. AI-related
demand is boosting business equipment investment (17.6%), fixed
business structures (7.0%), and intellectual property investment (6.6%).
Final sales to private domestic purchasers are expected to grow 4.7%.

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 266: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

### Page 5

Related articles

## Document 290: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

Recent gains in global share 
markets stalled overnight, 
with losses across the US, 
Europe and much of Asia 
as investors paused after 
a strong run higher in risk 
assets. 
The FOMC’s Minutes 
reinforced the hawkish 
tone accompanying the 
Fed’s September rate hike, 
with most participants still 
viewing further tightening as 
appropriate and almost all 
seeing inflation risks tilted to 
the upside at the time of the 
meeting.
Treasury yields were mixed 
despite the hawkish Minutes, 
with strong demand at a 
10-year auction helping 
stabilise long-end yields 
and reinforcing a broader 
steepening of the curve. 
Oil prices remained elevated 
amid competing signals 
from the Middle East, with 
recovering crude flows offset 
by reports of increased 
attacks on shipping through 
the Strait of Hormuz.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Share markets generally stalled across the globe. In the US,
stocks partially retraced gains from earlier in the week, with
the S&P 500 (–0.2%), Dow Jones (–0.7%) and NASDAQ
(–0.2%) all declining. Stocks fared worse across the pond,

with the Euro Stoxx 50 effectively unwinding all gains in
recent days (–1.5%), marked by chunky declines in Germany’s
DAX (–1.4%) and France’s CAC (–1.2%), alongside a larger
decline in London’s FTSE 100 (–0.8%). Stocks were also
weaker across much of Asia, falling in Tokyo (–0.9%), Hong
Kong (–0.6%) and Seoul (–2.0%). The ASX 200 only edged
slightly lower, down –0.1%, but futures markets are pointing
to a weaker open this morning.
•  The treasury yield curve steepened overnight. Gains in the

shorter-end saw 2-year yields fall 3bps to 4.77% – this was
despite a strong lift in consumer inflation expectations and
a hawkish FOMC Minutes, with market pricing for further
rate hikes from the FOMC instead softening at the margin.
Meanwhile, elevated yields at the long end drew very strong
demand at the latest 10-year auction, stabilising 10-year
yields at around 5.28%.
•  Bonds were more varied elsewhere, with Bunds bull
steepening amid a steady long-end, while Gilts bear

steepened amid a 6bp lift in the 10-year. ACGBs were
broadly steady during local trading, with yields inching only
slightly higher, though that gave way to gains in futures
trading overnight. Swaps markets continue to have around
6-7bps priced in for the RBA’s November meeting, while a
rate hike in December is seen at closer to 50/50 odds.
•  The USD bounced back overnight as the DXY rallied to an
intraday high of 102.5 before settling 0.4% higher at around

102.3. The Euro led the way lower, falling 0.6% versus the
greenback to just under USD1.12, back around its recent
lows. The Sterling shared a similar fate, down 0.5% toward
the USD1.321 level, remaining largely rangebound between
USD1.32-1.33 since late September. The Yen bucked the
wider trend, holding broadly steady. The Aussie dollar
meanwhile fell 0.3% to around USD0.696, unwinding gains
from earlier in the week.
•  Crude oil prices remain elevated as markets try to evaluate

the net impact of opposing forces – signs of recovering flows
of oil out of the Middle East is a positive, but reports that
Iran has increased the number of attacks on ships transiting
the Strait of Hormuz has kept a floor under prices. Brent was
up 0.5% to US$101.1/bbl overnight, still unable to break
sustainably under the US$100/bbl mark as of yet. Copper
prices lifted 0.4% to US$14,476 on the LME, gains being
driven by risks of tighter supply amid strikes at a major

Chilean mine, though the turn in risk sentiment overnight and
firmer USD capped gains. Gold prices meanwhile fell 1.3% to
around US$4,111/oz.
International Data
In the US, the FOMC’s Minutes  from the September meeting
reinforced the hawkish sentiment present at the time of the
decision to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a midpoint
of 3.875%. “All” participants supported the decision to raise
rates in September, and “most” saw further tightening by

year-end as appropriate. Underscoring that view was the fact
that “almost all” participants saw inflation risks as tilted to the
upside. Importantly, the assessment of the inflation outlook at
the time had already anticipated a 0.2ppt downward revision
to PCE inflation from the BEA’s change in methodology, which
ended up being 0.3ppt in the event.
The NY Fed’s measure of consumer inflation expectations
for the year ahead meanwhile surprised to the upside, rising

from 3.58% in for to 3.90% in September, marking the highest
result so far this year. Expectations also nudged 0.1ppt higher
to 3.3% at the three-year horizon, though they were stable at
3.0% at the five-year horizon.
Local Data
The Westpac Economics New Zealand team published its
September Retail Spending Pulse , which found that retail
spending remained resilient in the face of hgher fuel prices
(see here).

## Document 421: ueh22359

Source PDF: all report pdf/ueh22359.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 21

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Largan Precision
Core weakness outweighs progress in AI optics
CPO progress is more tangible but substantial success is already priced in
We maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target price as erosion in the core smartphone
business outweighs CPO progress. The Q326 call confirmed that Largan's fiber array has
passed customer qualification although its customer qualified another vendor's prism
micro-lens assembly (PMLA) that Largan would now buy in. If Largan qualifies the full

FAU plus carrier assembly for 2H27, content rises but with more pass-through cost and
less margin upside. We roll our valuation to 2028E, the first full year of CPO production,
though 25% below the NT$6,660 close on October 8.
Full FAU assembly would lift content, but with more pass-through
We believe Largan's FA customer is one of two  module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27
program, which we estimate could reach 40% share. The program is still subject to

successful yield ramp and could move to metalens in 2028, which would reopen supplier
qualification. Assembling the full FAU could lift Largan's ASP to US$125-150 leveraging
its precision alignment and automation but the PMLA, V-groove and carrier parts would
be pass through that cuts into margin leverage. We expand our FAU TAM analysis
(Figure 9S u m m a r y  o f  F A U  d r i v e r s :  1 2 . 3 m n / 3 5 . 6 m n  b a s e  c a s e  i n  2 0 2 8 - 2 0 3 0 ) with FAU market in 2028/30 expanding to 12.3mn/35.6mn units, with Largan

base case set at 25% of the TAM.
Core smartphone business stays soft into 2027
Q326 sales of NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ/-11% YoY missedUBSe/Street +36%/+33% on
weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 7.2ppts QoQ to 42.2%, lowest in 13
years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to 17
year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69. We cut
Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed orders

after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up periscope
share and outgrew in Q3 (+1% YoY vs. -11% YoY). We cut 2027 from NT$77bn to NT
$67bn, before a recovery to NT$79bn in 2028 with the next periscope upgrade. We see
GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs pass
through in 2028 pricing.
Maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target
We cut 2026E/27E EPS by 14%/16% to NT$160/NT$180 and introduce 2028E EPS of

NT$220 for the core smartphone business. We value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x
2028E ex-CPO EPS of NT$220, the middle of Largan's 13–18x historical range) and CPO
option at NT$1,700 or US$7bn (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35, near the
top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-unit, US
$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. That credits it with
yields and margins it still needs to prove in a quarter when yields and energy costs cut

7.2ppts from its lens margin. At ~NT$6,660, the shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO
EPS, about twice our base case.

Core smartphone lens seeing weakness with declining
margins
Q326 sales was reported light at NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ missing UBSe/Street +36%/
+33% amid weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 720bp to 42.2%, lowest in
13 years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to
17 year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69.
Renewable-energy costs are structural under Largan's long-term power commitments

and rise with Apple's share of the mix; with 2027 pricing set, pass-through is more likely
in 2028.
We cut Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed
orders after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up
periscope share and outgrew in Q3 (flat vs. -11% YoY).  October revenue should be flat
MoM and November lower as the key customer has shortened its forecast after cutting
flagship orders in September. The spring-2027 model starts shipping in October but will

not offset the flagship decline.

CPO fiber array qualified, but rising pass-through as it upgrades to FAU
production
Largan's fiber array (FA) has passed qualification with a volume customer, and
management expects mass-production capacity by mid-2027 in a newly completed fab
dedicated to FA/FAU, using in-house equipment. The company's next step is to produce
the full detachable FAU mounted on a carrier, a board with three alignment beams that
sits on the optical engine's receptor. Largan can build FAUs now and says the customer

### Page 3

Largan Precision 12 October 2026 ab 3
would prefer it to supply the full assembly, but qualification is pending with the
customer preferring FAU and carrier sampled separately.
Even as its potential to supply the full FAU improve, Largan will buy in most of the
content. The customer has qualified another PMLA vendor and prefers semiconductor-
made optics; V-grooves and carrier beams and boards are outsourced and the customer
does not want Largan to make MT ferrules. Management described its role as closer to a

contractor and said margins hinge on yields, as the FA carries a high ASP but also a large
bill of materials.  Supplying the complete FAU could raise billed content, but a larger
purchased bill of materials also dilutes the relationship between revenue and value
added. Our full-FAU ASP assumption requires Largan to capture that invoice scope.
Next year's product is grating-coupled and works for both CPO and NPO while edge-
coupled designs are being explored with the customer. The current product does not go

directly to end customers, but management expects to work directly with end customers
on next-generation designs (likely 2028) as it progresses toward multi-row FAs.
To ramp production, Largan has bought several land parcels and will build new fabs from
scratch, with construction finishing around 2030. It may outsource some capacity in the
interim and said it cannot yet be sure capacity will meet demand.
Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes and even mix
of these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s
ASICs using 6.4Tb/s optical engines.

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030, 55% of 1.1mn 204.8T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028 and 8.9mn in 2030.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native

NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028 and 35.6mn in 2030. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn
market in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030). The expansion to 2030 comes from wider optical adoption
and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline from US

$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Largan's CPO opportunity
Our base case gives Largan the benefit of the doubt for successfully ramping up a full
FAU production and capturing 25% of the overall FAU TAM, yielding NT$35 of 2028E
EPS, about 16% of our 2028E lens EPS. A 25% share means 3.1mn FAUs in its first full
year of production: about 29% of NVIDIA-linked units and roughly three-quarters of the

allocation our checks suggest its module partner holds. We book these at the full US
$150 FAU price and 50% gross margin, allowing for an early-supply premium even
though the optics, V-grooves and carrier parts are bought in. That gives 2028E CPO sales
of US$462mn, rising to US$1.1bn and NT$84 of EPS in 2030 on a larger market.
Our bear case (15% share, 45% GM) gives NT$5 of CPO EPS in 2028E and NT$14 in
2030E; our bull case (35% share, 55% GM) gives NT$103 and NT$267. Each 5ppt of

share moves 2028E CPO EPS by ~NT$6.9 (~NT$340/share at 49x), and each 5ppt of
gross margin by ~NT$4.6 (~NT$225/share).  If gross margin were 35% rather than 50%,
holding operating expenses at 12% of sales, EPS would fall to approximately NT$21. A
next-generation shift in lens or coupling design could also change content and
alignment requirements including a potential metalens design which could invite in
different suppliers.
Our 2030 base case implies ~8.9mn Largan FAUs a year (21mn in the upside case),

though with risks around competition in a crowded field, yields and technology. A shift
to semiconductor-made optics or metalens would loosen alignment tolerances and
erode the precision premium behind Largan's pitch. The upside lies in direct work with
cloud providers on multi-row FAs from 2028, where Largan's multi-layer alignment is
most differentiated.

Valuation and investment implications
We roll our valuation forward to 2028E and value Largan on a sum of the parts. We

value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x 2028E EPS, the middle of Largan's 13–18x
historical range) and CPO at NT$1,700 (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35,
near the top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-
unit, US$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. The 49x
multiple also assigns a substantial value to a business whose complete assembly still
requires qualification and whose margins still need to demonstrate can reach similar

levels to its mature smartphone business. At ~NT$6,660 recent share price level, the
shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO EPS, about twice our base case.
Upside risks include faster full-FAU qualification, firm volume commitments from
additional customers, stronger lens demand and better yields. Near-term catalysts are
the customer's capacity forecast and its visit to the new fab in the coming month. A
large forecast or faster FAU plus carrier qualification would be upside risks to our sell.

Forecast returns
Forecast price appreciation -24.9%
Forecast dividend yield 1.2%
Forecast stock return -23.7%
Market return assumption 6.3%
Forecast excess return -30.0%
Company Description
Founded in 1987, Largan Precision (Largan) is one of the world's leading manufacturers of
lenses used in cameras and IT products. It specialises in plastic/hybrid lenses and, to a lesser
extent, glass lenses and modules. In 2022, 20MP+ camera lenses were 10-20% of Largan's

shipment mix, 10MP lenses were 50-60%, 8MP lenses were 0-10% and other lenses were
30-40%.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business.
We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from smartphone OEM
clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a slower-than-
expected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success below the

market's elevated expectations.
We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-expected CPO qualification process at Largan.
In addition, if the company demonstrates exceptionally efficient and highly accurate
automation capabilities, it could support greater market share gains within the industry than
we currently anticipate.

Quantitative Research Review
UBS Global Research publishes a quantitative assessment of its analysts' responses to certain questions about the likelihood of an occurrence
of a number of short term factors in a product known as the 'Quantitative Research Review'.  The views for this month can be found below.
Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 447: ‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #labour #rba #productivity
‘Cost-Plus’ Costs
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:40 October 16 2026 Share
Entrenched expectations that cost increases can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against, but only indirectly.
The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices card-acceptance costs that had previously
been surcharged – and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-plus’ approach to pricing.

### Page 2

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused considerable upset this month, even though it is a
reversion to historical and international practice. The card schemes used to impose “no-surcharge”
rules, until the RBA banned these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The turnaround
represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people
to choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer made sense in a largely cashless
economy. In any case, least-cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in the hands of
the merchants who actually bear the cost.

In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker price, leaving the total cost to the
consumer unchanged, just more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers have
absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated
that around 16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though, some firms have lifted their
prices by more than any reasonable estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway coffee has been the centre of
consumer discontent.

Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main instigator of this month’s slump in
consumer sentiment. Petrol prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and the before-
and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week shows that higher interest rates also contributed
to the decline.

In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses
passing on increased costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to make that increase stick
– is exactly the kind of inflationary environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been subjected
to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel
to water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which for decades were a reliable source of cost
reductions, are nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins intact, demonstrates that
demand is not weak enough to prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations might be
higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.

To the extent they can pass on these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was strong
enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages growth has been moderate for some
years, though the above-average increase in award wages this year will add a little. Not all firms will
seek to pass these cost increases on, but some will, including because they have limited scope to
absorb the cost increase into margins.
A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it limits the imagination away from finding
alternative, efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with recent academic research
suggesting that firms form expectations and set prices according to what is happening in their own
industry and experience, while putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central bank
credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive
sector when supply shocks abound.

### Page 3

This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair Work Commission did hand down an
above-average increase in award wages this year and this was probably passed through to prices in
hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base wages growth has been moderate for some years.
Business surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue lately. This is why we were
puzzled by the passage in the RBA minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported in
business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU)
has risen. It is a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to overshoot your forecasts, as
the latest labour market data have done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.

Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do not have the market power, though coffee
may be an exception because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And as noted above,
large firms with everyday-low-price strategies will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply
chain.

Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on. Some observers might see this as corporate
greed, but another way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality. It turns out that cost-
plus pricing is especially common in areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated a lot in recent years.

There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The failures of so many building firms a few years
ago, squeezed between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and fixed-price contracts, were
a case in point. Normally, there is a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect firms never to pass cost increases
through to prices.

The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits the imagination away from finding responses
that might save costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost efficiency. It was
equally limiting in times of soft demand: in the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a
belief that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they could not possibly accept cost
increases, including in wages. Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be consistent
with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle of a central bank governor complaining that wages
growth was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to economise on it and thereby boost
productivity?

A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing
as the ‘inflation expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation, but it is related. The
stronger the tendency towards cost-plus pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the distinction matters. The recent academic
literature includes some fascinating empirical evidence from New Zealand that suggests that beliefs
about current and future overall inflation have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are shaped less by economy-wide inflation
developments than by what is happening to their costs and in their industry.

### Page 4

Related articles
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot like firms (in aggregate) setting prices
according to expected inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something quite different is going on.
If firms’ beliefs about future economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing decisions, then
neither does the credibility of a central bank’s commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism
to get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand enough to convince firms that cost-plus
will not fly in the market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-
sensitive sector when supply shocks abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade or even
self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.

The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can be reduced. That is more likely to be a
question for regulators than for central banks.
